The [Gilbert Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilbert_Islands) (now known as [Kiribati](16674.md)) and the Ellice Islands (now known as [Tuvalu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuvalu)) came under Britain's sphere of influence in the late 19th century.[^cite_note-TAHNPT-219][^cite_note-cinderella1-220]

French Catholic missionaries arrived on Tahiti in 1834; their expulsion in 1836 caused France to send a gunboat in 1838. In 1842, Tahiti and [Tahuata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahuata) were declared a [French protectorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protectorate), to allow Catholic missionaries to work undisturbed.[^cite_note-KMLA1889-221] On 24 September 1853, under orders from [Napoleon III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleon_III), Admiral [Febvrier Despointes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auguste_Febvrier_Despointes) took formal possession of New Caledonia.[^cite_note-ped-222]

The Spanish explorer [Alonso de Salazar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alonso_de_Salazar) landed in the Marshall Islands in 1529. They were named by [Krusenstern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Johann_von_Krusenstern), after English explorer [John Marshall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Marshall_\(British_captain\)), who visited them together with [Thomas Gilbert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Gilbert_\(captain\)) in 1788, en route from [Botany Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Botany_Bay) to [Canton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guangzhou) (two ships of the [First Fleet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Fleet)). In 1905, the British government transferred some administrative responsibility over southeast New Guinea to Australia (which renamed the area "[Territory of Papua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territory_of_Papua)"), and in 1906, transferred all remaining responsibility to Australia. The Marshall Islands were claimed by Spain in 1874. Germany established colonies in New Guinea in 1884, and [Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Samoa) in 1900. The United States also expanded into the Pacific, beginning with [Baker Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baker_Island) and [Howland Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howland_Island) in 1857, and with Hawaiʻi becoming a [US territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territory_of_Hawaii) in 1898. Disagreements between the US, the UK, and Germany over Samoa led to the [Tripartite Convention of 1899](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tripartite_Convention_\(1899\)).[^cite_note-amesagrey-223]

### Modern history

Main articles: [Pacific War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_War), [Military history of Oceania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_Oceania), [Military history of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_New_Zealand), [Military history of Australia during World War I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_Australia_during_World_War_I), [Military history of Australia during World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_Australia_during_World_War_II), [History of Guam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Guam), [History of Australia (1901–1945)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Australia_\(1901%E2%80%931945\)), and [Decolonisation of Oceania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decolonisation_of_Oceania)



New Zealand troops land on [Vella Lavella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vella_Lavella), in [Solomon Islands](265083.md)

One of the first land offensives in Oceania was the [occupation of German Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupation_of_German_Samoa) in August 1914 by [New Zealand forces](21359.md). The campaign to take Samoa ended without bloodshed after over 1,000 New Zealanders landed on the German colony. Australian forces attacked [German New Guinea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_New_Guinea) in September 1914. A company of Australians and a British warship besieged the Germans and their colonial subjects, ending with a German surrender.[^cite_note-awm.gov.au-224]

The [attack on Pearl Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attack_on_Pearl_Harbor) by the Japanese [Imperial General Headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_General_Headquarters),[^cite_note-pearl17ff-225][^cite_note-operat1315-226] was a surprise [military strike](897238.md) conducted by the [Imperial Japanese Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_Japanese_Navy) against the United States [naval base](291782.md) at [Pearl Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pearl_Harbor), Hawaii, on the morning of 7 December 1941. The attack led to the [United States' entry into World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_the_United_States_during_World_War_II). The Japanese subsequently invaded New Guinea, the Solomon Islands, and other Pacific islands. The Japanese were turned back at the [Battle of the Coral Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_the_Coral_Sea) and the [Kokoda Track campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kokoda_Track_campaign) before they were finally defeated in 1945. Some of the most prominent Oceanic battlegrounds were the [Battle of Bita Paka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Bita_Paka), the [Solomon Islands campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solomon_Islands_campaign), the [Air raids on Darwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombing_of_Darwin), the [Kokoda Track](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kokoda_Track_campaign), and the [Borneo campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borneo_campaign).[^cite_note-decode32-227][^cite_note-reconcil7-228] The United States fought the [Battle of Guam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Guam_\(1944\)) from 21 July to 10 August 1944, to recapture the island from [Japanese military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_military) occupation.[^cite_note-nyt1997-229]

Australia and New Zealand became [dominions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Dominions) in the 20th century, adopting the [Statute of Westminster Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statute_of_Westminster_1931) in 1942 and 1947, respectively. In 1946, Polynesians were granted French citizenship, and the islands' status was changed to an [overseas territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_administrative_territorial_entity); the islands' name was changed in 1957 to *Polynésie Française* ([French Polynesia](10737.md)). Hawaii became a [U.S. state](18618239.md) in 1959. [Fiji](10707.md) and [Tonga](30158.md) became independent in 1970. On 1 May 1979, in recognition of the evolving political status of the [Marshall Islands](19147.md), the United States recognised the constitution of the Marshall Islands and the establishment of the government of the [Republic of the Marshall Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_the_Marshall_Islands). The South Pacific Forum was founded in 1971, and it became the [Pacific Islands Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Islands_Forum) in 2000.[^cite_note-awm.gov.au-224]

## Geography

See also: [List of Oceanian countries by population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Oceanian_countries_by_population) and [List of sovereign states and dependent territories in Oceania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_and_dependent_territories_in_Oceania)



[Aoraki / Mount Cook](20212.md), located on the [South Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Island) of New Zealand



[Puncak Jaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puncak_Jaya) / *Carstensz Pyramid*, highest summit in Oceania

Under a four subregion model, the islands of Oceania extend to New Guinea in the west, the [Bonin Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bonin_Islands) in the northwest, the [Hawaiian Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawaiian_Islands) in the northeast, [Easter Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easter_Island) and [Sala y Gómez Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isla_Salas_y_G%C3%B3mez) in the east, and [Macquarie Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macquarie_Island) in the south. Excluded under most definitions of Oceania are the Pacific landmasses of [Taiwan](25734.md), the [Ryukyu Islands](8458939.md), and the [Japanese archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_archipelago), which are all on the margins of Asia, as well as the [Aleutian Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleutian_Islands) and other Alaskan or Canadian islands.[^cite_note-nz-115][^cite_note-ev-145] In its periphery, Oceania's islands would sprawl [28 degrees north](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/28th_parallel_north) to the Bonin Islands in the [Northern Hemisphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Hemisphere), and [55 degrees south](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/55th_parallel_south) to Macquarie Island in the [Southern Hemisphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Hemisphere).[^cite_note-race1750-230]

Oceanian islands are of four basic types: continental islands, [high islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_island), [coral reefs](87410.md), and uplifted [coral platforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coral_island). High islands are of volcanic origin, and many contain active volcanoes. Among these are [Bougainville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bougainville_Island), Hawaiʻi, and Solomon Islands.[^cite_note-GillespieClague2009-231]

Oceania is one of eight terrestrial [biogeographic realms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biogeographic_realm), which constitute the major ecological regions of the planet. Related to these concepts are [Near Oceania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Near_Oceania), that part of western [Island Melanesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Island_Melanesia) which has been inhabited for tens of millennia, and [Remote Oceania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remote_Oceania), which is more recently settled. Although the majority of the Oceanian islands lie in the South Pacific, a few of them are not restricted to the Pacific Ocean – [Kangaroo Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kangaroo_Island) and [Ashmore and Cartier Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashmore_and_Cartier_Islands), for instance, are situated in the [Southern Ocean](20611428.md) and [Indian Ocean](14580.md), respectively, and [Tasmania's west coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Coast,_Tasmania) faces the Southern Ocean.[^cite_note-othero5-232] The coral reefs of the South Pacific are low-lying structures that have built up on basaltic lava flows under the ocean's surface. One of the most dramatic is the [Great Barrier Reef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Barrier_Reef) off northeastern Australia with chains of reef patches. A second island type formed of coral is the uplifted coral platform, which is usually slightly larger than the low coral islands. Examples include [Banaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banaba_Island) (formerly Ocean Island) and [Makatea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makatea) in the Tuamotu group of [French Polynesia](10737.md).[^cite_note-britta-233][^cite_note-abcnet22613-234]

### Regions



A map of Oceania from the CIA World Factbook

[Micronesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Micronesia), which lies north of the equator and west of the [International Date Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Date_Line), includes the [Mariana Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariana_Islands) in the northwest, the [Caroline Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caroline_Islands) in the centre, the [Marshall Islands](19147.md) to the west and the islands of [Kiribati](16674.md) in the southeast.[^cite_note-AAE-235][^cite_note-LalFortune2000p63-236]

[Melanesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melanesia), to the southwest, includes [New Guinea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Guinea), the world's second largest island after [Greenland](12118.md) and by far the largest of the Pacific islands. The other main Melanesian groups from north to south are the [Bismarck Archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bismarck_Archipelago), the [Solomon Islands](265083.md), the [Santa Cruz Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Cruz_Islands), [Vanuatu](32443.md), [Fiji](10707.md), and [New Caledonia](21342.md).[^cite_note-West2009-237]

[Polynesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polynesia), stretching from Hawaii in the north to New Zealand in the south, also encompasses [Tuvalu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuvalu), [Tokelau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tokelau), [Samoa](27238.md), [Tonga](30158.md), and the [Kermadec Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kermadec_Islands) to the west, the [Cook Islands](7067.md), [Society Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Society_Islands) and [Austral Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austral_Islands) in the centre, and the [Marquesas Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marquesas_Islands), the [Tuamotus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuamotus), [Mangareva Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangareva_Islands), and [Easter Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easter_Island) to the east.[^cite_note-DunfordRidgell1996-238]

[Australasia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australasia) comprises Australia, New Zealand, and neighbouring islands in the Pacific Ocean. Along with India, most of Australasia lies on the [Indo-Australian Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Australian_Plate) with the latter occupying the Southern area. It is flanked by the Indian Ocean to the west and the [Southern Ocean](20611428.md) to the south.[^cite_note-voyage6-239][^cite_note-auxter-240]

### Geology

Main articles: [Geography of Fiji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Fiji), [Geology of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geology_of_New_Zealand), [Geology of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geology_of_Australia), and [Geography of Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Samoa)



The [Pacific Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Plate) comprises most of Oceania, excluding [Australasia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australasia) and the western portion of [Melanesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melanesia).

The [Pacific Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Plate), which makes up most of Oceania, is an oceanic [tectonic plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plate_tectonics) that lies beneath the Pacific Ocean. At 103 million square kilometres (40,000,000 sq mi), it is the largest tectonic plate. The plate contains an interior [hot spot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hotspot_\(geology\)) forming the [Hawaiian Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawaiian_Islands).[^cite_note-tecto-241] It is almost entirely [oceanic crust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceanic_crust).[^cite_note-platetec2010-242] The oldest member disappearing by way of the [plate tectonics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plate_tectonics) cycle is early-[Cretaceous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cretaceous) (145 to 137 million years ago).[^cite_note-ageof-243]

Australia became part of the [Indo-Australian Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Australian_Plate) 45 to 40 million years ago, and this is in the process of separating again, with the [Australian Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Plate) being relevant to Oceania.[^cite_note-244] It is the lowest, flattest, and oldest landmass on Earth[^cite_note-distinct227-245] and it has had a relatively stable geological history. Geological forces such as [tectonic uplift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tectonic_uplift) of mountain ranges or clashes between tectonic plates occurred mainly in Australia's early history, when it was still a part of [Gondwana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gondwana). Australia is situated in the middle of the tectonic plate, has occasional middle-sized earthquakes, and currently has no active volcanism (but some volcanoes in southeast Australia erupted within the last 10,000 years).[^cite_note-ag-246]

The [geology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geology) of New Zealand is noted for its [volcanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volcanic) activity, [earthquakes](10106.md), and [geothermal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geothermal_activity) [areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geothermal_areas_in_New_Zealand) because of its position on the boundary of the [Australian Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Plate) and Pacific Plates. Much of the basement rock of New Zealand was once part of the super-continent of Gondwana, along with South America, Africa, [Madagascar](18964.md), India, Antarctica, and Australia. The rocks that now form the continent of [Zealandia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zealandia_\(continent\)) were nestled between Eastern Australia and [Western Antarctica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Antarctica).[^cite_note-tearaz-247]

The Australia-New Zealand continental fragment of Gondwana split from the rest of Gondwana in the [late Cretaceous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Cretaceous) time (95–90 Ma). By 75 Ma, Zealandia was essentially separate from Australia and Antarctica, although only shallow seas might have separated Zealandia and Australia in the north. The [Tasman Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tasman_Sea), and part of Zealandia, then locked together with Australia to form the [Australian Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Plate) (40 Ma), and a new plate boundary was created between the Australian Plate and Pacific Plate.

Most islands in the Pacific are [high islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_islands) ([volcanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volcanic) islands), such as [Easter Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easter_Island), [American Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Samoa), and [Fiji](10707.md), among others, having peaks up to 1300 m rising abruptly from the shore.[^cite_note-Factbook-Fiji-248] The [Northwestern Hawaiian Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_Hawaiian_Islands) were formed c. 7 to 30 million years ago, as [shield volcanoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shield_volcano) over the same volcanic [hotspot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hotspot_\(geology\)) that formed the [Emperor Seamounts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emperor_Seamounts) to the north and the Main Hawaiian Islands to the south.[^cite_note-geochr-249] Hawaii's tallest mountain [Mauna Kea](267366.md) is 4,205 m (13,796 ft) above mean sea level.[^cite_note-usgsgov-250]

### Flora

Main articles: [Geography of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Australia), [Environment of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environment_of_New_Zealand), [Flora of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flora_of_Australia), [Flora of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flora_of_New_Zealand), [Endemism in the Hawaiian Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemism_in_the_Hawaiian_Islands), and [Domesticated plants of Austronesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domesticated_plants_of_Austronesia)



New Zealand countryside



[Uluru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uluru) (Ayers Rock) in [Central Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Australia)

The most diverse country of Oceania when it comes to the environment is Australia, with tropical [rainforests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rainforest) in the north-east, mountain ranges in the south-east, south-west, and east, and a desert in the centre.[^cite_note-envigov2012-251] [Desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deserts_of_Australia) or semi-arid land commonly known as the [outback](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outback) makes up by far the largest portion of land.[^cite_note-portrait-252] The [coastal uplands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Australian_temperate_forests) and a [belt of Brigalow grasslands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigalow_Belt) lie between the coast and the mountains, while inland of the dividing range are large areas of grassland.[^cite_note-drivers375-253] The northernmost point of the east coast is the tropical-rainforested [Cape York Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_York_Peninsula).[^cite_note-uplands-254][^cite_note-gdowns-255][^cite_note-mulgas-256][^cite_note-tempera-257][^cite_note-savanna-258]

Prominent features of the Australian flora are adaptations to [aridity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aridity) and fire, which include [scleromorphy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sclerophyll) and [serotiny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serotiny). These adaptations are common in species from the large and well-known families [Proteaceae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proteaceae) (*[Banksia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banksia)*), [Myrtaceae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myrtaceae) (*[Eucalyptus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eucalyptus)* – gum trees), and [Fabaceae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabaceae) (*[Acacia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acacia)* – wattle). The flora of Fiji, [Solomon Islands](265083.md), [Vanuatu](32443.md), and [New Caledonia](21342.md) is [tropical dry forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_dry_forest), with [tropical vegetation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_vegetation) that includes [palm trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palm_trees), *[Premna protrusa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premna_protrusa)*, *[Psydrax odorata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psydrax_odorata)*, *[Gyrocarpus americanus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gyrocarpus_americanus)*, and *[Derris trifoliata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derris_trifoliata)*.[^cite_note-mostvb-259]

New Zealand's landscape ranges from the [fjord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fjord)-like sounds of the southwest to the tropical beaches of the far north. The South Island is dominated by the [Southern Alps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Alps). There are 18 peaks of more than 3000 metres (9800 ft) in the South Island. All summits over 2,900 m are within the Southern Alps, a chain that forms the backbone of the South Island; the highest peak of which is [Aoraki / Mount Cook](20212.md), at 3,754 metres (12,316 ft). Earthquakes are common, though usually not severe, averaging 3,000 per year.[^cite_note-newzat-260] There is a wide variety of native trees, adapted to all the various microclimates in New Zealand.[^cite_note-vascplant-261]

In Hawaii, one endemic plant, *[Brighamia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brighamia)*, now requires hand-pollination because its natural pollinator is presumed to be extinct.[^cite_note-plantprop-262] The two species of *Brighamia&#x20;*– *B. rockii* and *B. insignis&#x20;*– are represented in the wild by around 120 individual plants. To ensure these plants set seed, biologists rappel down 910-metre (3,000 ft) cliffs to brush pollen onto their stigmas.[^cite_note-forgotpol-263]

The area of primary forest decreased in Oceania by 17 000 hectares per year in 1990–2000, 25 600 ha per year in 2000–2015 and 20 200 ha per year in 2015–2025.[^cite_note-264]

### Fauna

Main articles: [Fauna of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fauna_of_Australia), [Birds of Australia](160118.md), [Birds of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birds_of_New_Zealand), [Fauna of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fauna_of_New_Zealand), [Mammals of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mammals_of_Australia), [List of birds of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_birds_of_Australia), [List of birds of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_birds_of_New_Zealand), and [Domesticated animals of Austronesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domesticated_animals_of_Austronesia)



The [Pacific robin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_robin) inhabits the islands of the south western Pacific.

[^cite_note-IUCN-265]

The aptly named [Pacific kingfisher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_kingfisher) is found in the Pacific Islands,[^cite_note-handbirds-266] as is the [Red-vented bulbul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red-vented_bulbul),[^cite_note-icunet22717-267] [Polynesian starling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polynesian_starling),[^cite_note-hawaiibirds-268] [Brown goshawk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brown_goshawk),[^cite_note-birdsback-269] [Pacific Swallow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Swallow)[^cite_note-Turner-270] and the [Cardinal myzomela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinal_myzomela), among others.[^cite_note-cardinal-271] Birds breeding on [Pitcairn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pitcairn) include the [fairy tern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairy_tern), [common noddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_noddy), and [red-tailed tropicbird](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red-tailed_tropicbird). The [Pitcairn reed warbler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pitcairn_reed_warbler), endemic to Pitcairn Island, was added to the [endangered species](20110668.md) list in 2008.[^cite_note-icunred181023-272]

Native to Hawaii is the [Hawaiian crow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawaiian_crow), which has been [extinct in the wild](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extinct_in_the_wild) since 2002.[^cite_note-forestbirds-273] The [brown tree snake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brown_tree_snake) is native to northern and eastern coasts of Australia, Papua New Guinea, Guam, and Solomon Islands.[^cite_note-btsnakegov-274] Native to Australia, New Guinea and proximate islands are [birds of paradise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birds_of_paradise), [honeyeaters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honeyeater), [Australasian treecreeper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australasian_treecreeper), [Australasian robin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australasian_robin), [kingfishers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingfisher), [butcherbirds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butcherbird), and [bowerbirds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bowerbird).[^cite_note-systax-275][^cite_note-Steadman_2006-276]

A unique feature of Australia's fauna is the relative scarcity of native [placental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Placentalia) mammals, and dominance of the [marsupials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marsupial) – a group of mammals that raise their young in a [pouch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pouch_\(marsupial\)), including the [macropods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macropodidae), [possums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phalangeriformes), and [dasyuromorphs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dasyuromorphia). The [passerines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passerine) of Australia, also known as songbirds or perching birds, include [wrens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wren), the [magpie group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artamidae), [thornbills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acanthiza), [corvids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corvid), [pardalotes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pardalote), [lyrebirds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyrebird).[^cite_note-ioc-277] Predominant bird species in the country include the [Australian magpie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_magpie), [Australian raven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_raven), the [pied currawong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pied_currawong), [crested pigeons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crested_pigeon) and the [laughing kookaburra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laughing_kookaburra).[^cite_note-encycwild-278] The [koala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koala), [emu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emu), [platypus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Platypus) and [kangaroo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kangaroo) are [national animals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_animals) of Australia,[^cite_note-CoatArms-279] and the [Tasmanian devil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tasmanian_devil) is also one of the well-known animals in the country.[^cite_note-tassie-280] The [goanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goanna) is a [predatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Predatory) lizard native to the [Australian mainland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_mainland).[^cite_note-dangerc-281]

The birds of New Zealand evolved into an avifauna that included a large number of [endemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemism) species. As an island archipelago, New Zealand accumulated bird diversity, and when Captain James Cook arrived in the 1770s, he noted that the [bird song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bird_vocalization) was deafening. The mix includes species with unusual biology, such as the [kākāpō](89074.md), which is the world's only flightless, nocturnal, [lek-breeding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lek_mating) parrot, but also many species that are similar to neighbouring land areas. Some of the more well known and distinctive bird species in New Zealand are the [kiwi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiwi_\(bird\)), [kea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kea), [takahē](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takah%C4%93), kākāpō, [mohua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellowhead_\(bird\)), [tūī](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C5%AB%C4%AB), and the [bellbird](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_bellbird).[^cite_note-assemb152-282] The [tuatara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuatara) is a notable [reptile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reptile) endemic to New Zealand.[^cite_note-vicvag32-283]

Australia, New Zealand, New Guinea, Wallacea, and the islands of the Pacific Ocean collectively possess 42% of the world's [parrot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parrot) species, including half of all [Critically Endangered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Critically_Endangered) parrots, many of which are [endemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemic) to the region.[^cite_note-Olah2018-284]

### Climate

Main articles: [Climate of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Australia), [Climate of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_New_Zealand), and [Climate of Hawaii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Hawaii)



August 2011 winter's snowfall in [Dunedin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunedin), [Otago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otago)

The Pacific Islands are ruled by a [tropical rainforest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_rainforest) and [tropical savanna climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_savanna_climate). In the tropical and subtropical Pacific, the [El Niño Southern Oscillation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Ni%C3%B1o_Southern_Oscillation) (ENSO) affects weather conditions.[^cite_note-noaagov-285] In the tropical western Pacific, the [monsoon](57630.md) and the related [wet season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wet_season) during the summer months contrast with dry winds in the winter which blow over the ocean from the Asian landmass.[^cite_note-allenp2009-286] November is the only month in which all the [tropical cyclone basins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_cyclone_basins) are active.[^cite_note-AOML_FAQ_G1-287]

To the southwest of the region, in the Australian landmass, the climate is mostly desert or [semi-arid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-arid_climate), with the southern coastal corners having a [temperate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temperateness) climate, such as [oceanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceanic_climate) and [humid subtropical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_subtropical) climate in the east coast and [Mediterranean climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_climate) in the west. The northern parts of the country have a [tropical climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_climate).[^cite_note-climatea-288] Snow falls frequently on the highlands near the east coast, in the states of [Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_\(Australia\)), [New South Wales](21654.md), [Tasmania](29944.md) and in the [Australian Capital Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Capital_Territory).[^cite_note-precip376-289]

Most regions of New Zealand belong to the temperate zone with a [maritime climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maritime_climate) ([Köppen climate classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification): Cfb) characterised by four distinct seasons. Conditions vary from extremely wet on the [West Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Coast,_New_Zealand) of the [South Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Island) to almost [semi-arid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-arid_climate) in [Central Otago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Otago) and [subtropical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subtropical) in [Northland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Auckland_Peninsula).[^cite_note-enviclim-290][^cite_note-niwaco-291] Snow falls in New Zealand's [South Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Island) and at higher altitudes in the [North Island](62046.md). It is extremely rare at sea level in the North Island.[^cite_note-tele141116-292]

Hawaii, although being in the [tropics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropics), experiences many different climates, depending on latitude and its geography. The [island of Hawaii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawaii_\(island\)), for example, hosts 4 (out of 5 in total) climate groups on a surface as small as 10,430 km<sup>2</sup> (4,028 sq mi) according to the Köppen climate types: tropical, arid, temperate, and polar. The Hawaiian Islands receive most of their precipitation during the winter months (October to April).[^cite_note-loveb20120703-293] A few islands in the northwest, such as [Guam](11974.md), are susceptible to [typhoons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Typhoon) in the wet season.[^cite_note-pubaff042700-294]

The highest recorded temperature in Oceania occurred in [Oodnadatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oodnadatta), South Australia (2 January 1960) and in [Onslow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onslow,_Western_Australia), Western Australia (13 January 2022), both of which recorded a Temperature of 50.7 °C (123.3 °F).[^cite_note-bomgovdai-295] The lowest temperature ever recorded in Oceania was −25.6 °C (−14.1 °F), at [Ranfurly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranfurly,_New_Zealand) in Otago in 1903, with a more recent temperature of −21.6 °C (−6.9 °F) recorded in 1995 in nearby [Ophir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ophir,_New_Zealand).[^cite_note-herald120611-296] [Pohnpei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pohnpei) of the [Senyavin Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senyavin_Islands) in [Micronesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Micronesia) is the wettest settlement in Oceania, and one of the wettest places on earth, with annual recorded rainfall exceeding 7,600 mm (300 in) each year in certain mountainous locations.[^cite_note-werigu-297] [The Big Bog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Bog,_Maui) on the island of Maui is the wettest place, receiving an average 10,271 mm (404.4 in) each year.[^cite_note-matology105-298]

## Politics

### Australia

Main article: [Politics of Australia](4711662.md)



Charles is [Head of the Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_the_Commonwealth) and King of five Oceanian countries: Australia, New Zealand, [Papua New Guinea](22943.md), [Solomon Islands](265083.md), and [Tuvalu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuvalu).

Australia is a [federal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federalism) [parliamentary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_system) [constitutional monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_monarchy)[^cite_note-ausparliwork-299] with [Charles III](125248.md) at its apex as the [King of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Australia), a role that is distinct from his position as monarch of the other [Commonwealth realms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_realm). The King is represented in Australia by the [Governor-General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor-General_of_Australia) at the federal level and by the [Governors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governors_of_the_Australian_states) at the state level, who by convention act on the advice of his ministers.[^cite_note-Davison_pp287–8-300][^cite_note-govgenaus-301] There are two major political groups that usually form government, federally and in the states: the [Australian Labor Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Labor_Party) and the [Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalition_\(Australia\)) which is a formal grouping of the [Liberal Party](18453.md) and its minor partner, the [National Party](21927.md).[^cite_note-glosselec-302][^cite_note-statepar-303] Within Australian political culture, the Coalition is considered [centre-right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre-right) and the Labor Party is considered [centre-left](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre-left).[^cite_note-govpol2013-304] The [Australian Defence Force](67538.md) is by far the largest military force in Oceania.[^cite_note-defenceann-305]

### New Zealand

Main article: [Politics of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_New_Zealand)

New Zealand is a [constitutional monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_monarchy) with a [parliamentary democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_democracy),[^cite_note-GG_constitution-306] although [its constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_New_Zealand) is [not codified](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uncodified_constitution).[^cite_note-Economist_factsheet-307] [Charles III](125248.md) is the [King of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_New_Zealand) and the [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state).[^cite_note-royalt1974-308] The King is represented by the [Governor-General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor-General_of_New_Zealand), whom he appoints on the [advice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advice_\(constitutional\)) of the [Prime Minister](106321.md).[^cite_note-rolegg-309] The [New Zealand Parliament](340639.md) holds [legislative power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_power) and consists of the King and the [House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_House_of_Representatives).[^cite_note-parliament_facts-310] A [parliamentary general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_New_Zealand) must be called no later than three years after the previous election.[^cite_note-eleccyc-311] New Zealand is identified as one of the world's most stable and well-governed states,[^cite_note-fragileind16-312][^cite_note-demoindex16-313] with high government transparency and among the lowest perceived levels of corruption.[^cite_note-transpint201116-314]

### Pacific Islands



Government building in the Samoan capital [Apia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apia) housing administrative ministerial offices

In [Samoan politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Samoa), the [Prime Minister of Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Samoa) is the [head of government](37570.md). The [1960 constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Samoa), which formally came into force with independence from New Zealand in 1962, builds on the British pattern of [parliamentary democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_democracy), modified to take account of Samoan customs. The national government (*malo*) generally controls the [legislative assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_Assembly_of_Samoa).[^cite_note-backnotes-315] [Politics of Tonga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Tonga) takes place in a framework of a [constitutional monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_monarchy), whereby the [King of Tonga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_of_Tonga) is the [Head of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_State).

[Fiji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Fiji) has a multiparty system with the [Prime Minister of Fiji](471842.md) as head of government. The [executive power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_\(government\)) is exercised by the government. [Legislative power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_power) is vested in both the government and the [Parliament of Fiji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Fiji). [Fiji](10707.md)'s [Head of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_State_of_Fiji) is the [President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Fiji).

In the [politics of Papua New Guinea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Papua_New_Guinea) the [Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Prime_Ministers_of_the_Papua_New_Guinea) is the head of government, and the head of state is [the monarch of the United Kingdom](47063.md), represented by a [Governor-General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor-General_of_Papua_New_Guinea). In [Kiribati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Kiribati), a [Parliamentary regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_system), the [President of Kiribati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Kiribati) is the head of state and government, and of a multi-party system.

[New Caledonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_New_Caledonia) remains an integral part of the [French Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Republic). Inhabitants of New Caledonia are [French citizens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_citizens). New Caledonia sends two representatives to the [French National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_National_Assembly) and two senators to the [French Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Senate).

[Hawaii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Hawaii) is dominated by the [Democratic Party](5043544.md). As codified in the [Hawaiian Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Hawaii), there are three [branches of government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Branches_of_government): executive, legislative, and judicial. The governor is elected statewide.

### Australia

Main article: [Economy of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Australia)



The [skyline of Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skyline_of_Sydney)

Australia and New Zealand are the only highly [developed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developed_country) independent nations in the region, although the [economy of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Australia) is by far the largest and most dominant economy in the region and one of the largest in the world. New Caledonia, Hawaiʻi ʻi, and French Polynesia are highly developed too, but are not [sovereign](28791.md) states. Australia's per-capita GDP is [higher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(PPP\)_per_capita) than that of the UK, Canada, Germany, and France in terms of [purchasing power parity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity).[^cite_note-unstat228-316] New Zealand is also one of the most globalised economies and depends greatly on international trade.[^cite_note-stand260511-317][^cite_note-guard150411-318]

The [Australian Securities Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Securities_Exchange) in [Sydney](27862.md) is the largest [stock exchange in Australia and in the South Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_stock_exchanges_in_Oceania).[^cite_note-if5com200514-319] In 2012, Australia was the 12th largest national economy by nominal GDP and the 19th-largest measured by [PPP-adjusted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity) GDP.[^cite_note-rbagov200514-320]

Mercer Quality of Living Survey ranks Sydney tenth in the world in terms of [quality of living](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quality_of_living),[^cite_note-mercer.com-321] making it one of the [most livable cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World's_most_livable_cities).[^cite_note-mercer2014-322] It is classified as an Alpha [World City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_City) by [GaWC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalization_and_World_Cities_Research_Network).[^cite_note-accgawc-323][^cite_note-urbanst-324] [Melbourne](17306237.md) also ranked highly in the [world's most liveable city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World's_Most_Livable_Cities) list,[^cite_note-melb181-325] and is a leading [financial centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_centre) in the [Asia–Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia%E2%80%93Pacific) region.[^cite_note-longfi041213-326][^cite_note-citindex-327]



Auckland's [central business district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auckland_CBD) at night in 2006

The majority of people living in Australia work in health care, retail and education sectors.[^cite_note-328] Australia boasts the largest amount of manufacturing in the region, [producing cars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive_industry_in_Australia), electrical equipment, machinery and [clothes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clothing_industry).

### New Zealand

Main article: [Economy of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_New_Zealand)

New Zealand's [economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_New_Zealand) is the [53rd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\))-largest in the world measured by [nominal gross domestic product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nominal_gross_domestic_product) (GDP) and [68th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(PPP\))-largest in the world measured by [purchasing power parity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity) (PPP). A major economic and cultural powerhouse of the [Southern Hemisphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Hemisphere), Auckland is ranked as a Beta+ world city by the [Globalization and World Cities Research Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalization_and_World_Cities_Research_Network). Auckland and [Wellington](33804.md) are frequently ranked among the [world's most liveable cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World's_most_liveable_cities), with Auckland being ranked [first in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Liveability_Ranking) according to the [Global Liveability Ranking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Liveability_Ranking).[^cite_note-businrank-329][^cite_note-mercer190214-330]

[New Zealand](4913064.md) has a large GDP for its population of 5.2 million, and sources of revenue are spread throughout the large island nation. The country has one of the most [globalised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalised) economies and depends greatly on international trade – mainly with [Australia](4689264.md), [Canada](5042916.md), [China](5405.md), the [European Union](9317.md), [Japan](15573.md), [Singapore](27318.md), [South Korea](27019.md) and the [United States](3434750.md). New Zealand's 1983 [Closer Economic Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Closer_Economic_Relations) agreement with Australia means that the economy aligns closely with [that of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Australia). In 2005, the [World Bank](36752.md) praised New Zealand as the most business-friendly country in the world.[^cite_note-SALE-331][^cite_note-IHT-332] The economy diversified and by 2008, tourism had become the single biggest generator of foreign exchange.[^cite_note-diversify-333] The New Zealand dollar is the 10th-most traded currency in the world.[^cite_note-BIS-334]

### Pacific Islands

Main articles: [Economy of Fiji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Fiji), [Economy of Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Samoa), and [Economy of Tonga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Tonga)



[Honolulu](13887.md) viewed from [Diamond Head](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diamond_Head,_Hawaii) crater

The overwhelming majority of people living in the Pacific islands work in the service industry, which includes tourism, education, and financial services. Oceania's largest export markets include Japan, China, the United States, and South Korea. The smallest Pacific nations rely on trade with Australia, New Zealand, and the United States for exporting goods and for accessing other products. Australia and New Zealand's trading arrangements are known as [Closer Economic Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Closer_Economic_Relations). Australia and New Zealand, along with other countries, are members of [Asia–Pacific Economic Cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia-Pacific_Economic_Cooperation) (APEC) and the [East Asia Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Asia_Summit) (EAS), which may become trade blocs in the future, particularly EAS.

The main produce from the Pacific is [copra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copra) or [coconut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coconut), but [timber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timber), [beef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beef), [palm oil](57561.md), [cocoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cocoa_bean), [sugar](27712.md), and [ginger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ginger) are also commonly grown across the [tropics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropics) of the Pacific. [Fishing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fishing) provides a major [industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industry_\(economics\)) for many of the smaller nations in the Pacific, although many [fishing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fishing) areas are exploited by other larger countries, namely Japan. [Natural Resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_Resources), such as [lead](17747.md), [zinc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zinc), [nickel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nickel), and [gold](12240.md), are mined in Australia and [Solomon Islands](265083.md). Oceania's largest export markets include Japan, China, the United States, India, South Korea, and the [European Union](9317.md).



Aerial view of [Bora Bora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bora_Bora), French Polynesia

Endowed with forest, mineral, and fish resources, [Fiji](10707.md) is one of the most developed of the Pacific island [economies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economics), though it remains a [developing country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developing_nation) with a large [subsistence agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subsistence_agriculture) sector.[^cite_note-ciafacfj-335] Agriculture accounts for 18% of [gross domestic product](12594.md), although it employed some 70% of the workforce as of 2001. [Sugar](27712.md) exports and the growing tourist industry are the major sources of foreign exchange. Sugar cane processing makes up one-third of industrial activity. [Coconuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coconut), [ginger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ginger), and [copra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copra) are also significant.

The history of Hawaii's economy can be traced through a succession of dominant industries; [sandalwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandalwood),[^cite_note-hawahis-336] [whaling](33557.md),[^cite_note-whalinhawa-337] sugarcane, [pineapple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pineapple), the military, tourism, and education.[^cite_note-capitagdf-338] Hawaiian exports include food and clothing. These industries play a small role in the Hawaiian economy, due to the shipping distance to viable markets, such as the West Coast of the contiguous U.S. The state's food exports include coffee, macadamia nuts, pineapple, livestock, sugarcane, and honey.[^cite_note-hawabee-339] As of 2015, Honolulu was ranked high on world livability rankings, and was also ranked as the 2nd safest city in the U.S.[^cite_note-MercerSurvey2015-340][^cite_note-KITVSafestCity-341]

### Tourism

Further information: [Tourism in Australia](274784.md) and [Tourism in New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_New_Zealand)



[Shangri-La's Fijian Resort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shangri-La's_Fijian_Resort)

Tourists mostly come from Japan, the United Kingdom, and the United States. [Fiji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Fiji#Tourism) currently attracts almost half a million tourists each year, more than a quarter of whom come from Australia. This has contributed \$1 billion or more to Fiji's economy since 1995, but the Government of Fiji likely underestimates these figures due to the invisible economy inside the tourism industry.

Vanuatu is widely recognised as one of the premier vacation destinations for [scuba divers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scuba_divers) wishing to explore [coral reefss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coral_reefs) of the South Pacific region. Tourism has been promoted, in part, by Vanuatu being the site of several reality-TV shows. The ninth season of the reality TV series *[Survivor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Survivor_\(US_TV_series\))* was filmed in Vanuatu, entitled *[Survivor: Vanuatu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Survivor:_Vanuatu) – Islands of Fire*. Two years later, Australia's *[Celebrity Survivor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celebrity_Survivor)* was filmed at the same location used by the U.S. version.[^cite_note-uatustats-342]



[Dandenong Ranges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dandenong_Ranges) in [Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_\(Australia\)) are popular among tourists.

[Tourism in Australia](274784.md) is an important component of the Australian economy. In the financial year 2014/15, [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism) represented 3% of [Australia](4689264.md)'s [GDP](12594.md) contributing A\$47.5 billion to the national economy.[^cite_note-ABS1011-343] In 2015, there were 7.4 million visitor arrivals.[^cite_note-touraus181029-344] Popular Australian destinations include the [Sydney Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Harbour) ([Sydney Opera House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Opera_House), [Sydney Harbour Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Harbour_Bridge), [Royal Botanic Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Botanic_Garden,_Sydney), etc.), [Gold Coast](59251.md) ([theme parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theme_parks) such as [Warner Bros. Movie World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Bros._Movie_World), [Dreamworld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dreamworld_\(Australian_theme_park\)) and [Sea World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_World_\(Australia\))), [Walls of Jerusalem National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walls_of_Jerusalem_National_Park) and [Mount Field National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Field_National_Park) in [Tasmania](29944.md), [Royal Exhibition Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Exhibition_Building) in [Melbourne](17306237.md), the [Great Barrier Reef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Barrier_Reef) in [Queensland](59051.md), [The Twelve Apostles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Twelve_Apostles_\(Victoria\)) in [Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_\(Australia\)), [Uluru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uluru) (Ayers Rock) and the [Australian outback](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_outback).[^cite_note-ausnatacc-345]

[Tourism in New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_New_Zealand) contributes [NZ\$](161585.md)7.3 billion (or 4%) of the country's [GDP](12594.md) in 2013, as well as directly supporting 110,800 full-time equivalent jobs (nearly 6% of New Zealand's workforce). International tourist spending accounted for 16% of New Zealand's export earnings (nearly NZ\$10 billion). International and domestic tourism contribute, in total, NZ\$24 billion to New Zealand's economy every year. [Tourism New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_New_Zealand), the country's official tourism agency, is actively promoting the country as a destination worldwide.[^cite_note-minisbusi-346] [Milford Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milford_Sound) in the South Island is acclaimed as New Zealand's most famous tourist destination.[^cite_note-SC-347]

In 2003 alone, according to state government data, there were over 6.4 million [visitors to the Hawaiian Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Hawaii) with expenditures of over \$10.6 billion.[^cite_note-DBEDT_2003-348] Due to the mild year-round weather, tourist travel is popular throughout the year. In 2011, Hawaiʻi saw increasing arrivals and share of foreign tourists from Canada, Australia, and China increasing 13%, 24% and 21% respectively from 2010.[^cite_note-hawasafe-349]

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Oceania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Oceania)

The predominant religion in Oceania is [Christianity](5211.md) (73%).[^cite_note-Christianity_in_Oceania-367][^cite_note-US_Dept_of_State_Background_Notes-368] A 2011 survey found that 92% in [Melanesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melanesia),[^cite_note-Christianity_in_Oceania-367] 93% in [Micronesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Micronesia)[^cite_note-Christianity_in_Oceania-367] and 96% in [Polynesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polynesia) described themselves as [Christians](18337522.md).[^cite_note-Christianity_in_Oceania-367] Traditional religions are often [animist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animism), and prevalent among traditional tribes is the belief in spirits (*masalai* in [Tok Pisin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tok_Pisin)) representing natural forces.[^cite_note-Anthropology-Cowan-Messengers_of_the_Gods-369] In the 2018 census, 37% of New Zealanders affiliated themselves with Christianity, and 48% declared no religion.[^cite_note-quickstats-370] In the 2016 Census, 52% of the Australian population declared some variety of Christianity and 30% stated "no religion".[^cite_note-reflect-371]

In recent Australian and New Zealand censuses, large proportions of the population say they belong to "[no religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion)" (which includes [atheism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheism), [agnosticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnosticism), [deism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deism), and [secular humanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secular_humanism)). In [Tonga](30158.md), everyday life is heavily influenced by [Polynesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polynesian_culture) traditions and especially by the Christian faith. The [Ahmadiyya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmadiyya) mosque in the Marshall Islands is the only mosque in Micronesia.[^cite_note-mosque2011-372] Another one in [Tuvalu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuvalu) belongs to the same sect. The [Baháʼí House of Worship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_House_of_Worship) in Tiapapata, [Samoa](27238.md), is one of seven designations administered in the [Baháʼí Faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith).

[Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) is a minority faith in Oceania. [Fiji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Fiji) has the highest percentage of Hindus in Oceania at 29.7%[^cite_note-373] In absolute numbers, [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Australia) has the largest population of Hindus in Oceania constituting 2.7% of the country's population.[^cite_note-374] In [New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_New_Zealand), Hindus form 2.65% of the population of.[^cite_note-375] [Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Samoa) also has a significant Hindu population.[^cite_note-376]

Other religions in the region include [Islam](6037917.md) and [Buddhism](3267529.md), which are prominent [minority religions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minority_religions?action=edit\&redlink=1) in Australia and New Zealand. [Judaism](15624.md), [Sikhism](27964.md) and [Jainism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism) are also present. Sir [Isaac Isaacs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isaac_Isaacs) was the first Australian-born [Governor General of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_General_of_Australia) and was the first Jewish vice-regal representative in the [British Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Empire).[^cite_note-377] [Prince Philip Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_Philip_Movement) is followed around [Yaohnanen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yaohnanen) village on the southern island of [Tanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanna_\(island\)) in [Vanuatu](32443.md).[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379]

- Holy's Innocent Anglican Church in Amberley, New Zealand

  Holy's Innocent Anglican Church in [Amberley, New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amberley,_New_Zealand)

- Saione, the church of the King, a Free Wesleyan Church in Kolomotuʻa, Tonga. Especially, British and American missionaries brought various Protestant denominations to Oceania.

Especially, British and American missionaries brought various Protestant denominations to Oceania.
