### Languages

Main article: [Languages of Oceania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Oceania)

Native languages of Oceania fall into three major geographic groups:

- The large [Austronesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austronesian_languages) [language family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language_family), with such languages as [Malay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malay_language) (Indonesian), and [Oceanic languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceanic_languages) such as [Gilbertese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilbertese_language), [Fijian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fijian_language), [Māori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C4%81ori_language), and [Hawaiʻian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawaiian_language)

- The [Aboriginal Australian languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aboriginal_Australian_languages), including the large [Pama–Nyungan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pama%E2%80%93Nyungan) family

- The [Papuan languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papuan_languages) of [New Guinea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Guinea) and neighbouring islands, including the large [Trans–New Guinea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans%E2%80%93New_Guinea) family

Immigrants brought their own languages to the region.[^cite_note-absgov20120621-380] Common non-indigenous languages include [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language) in Australia, New Zealand, Hawaii, and many other territories; [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language) in [New Caledonia](21342.md), [French Polynesia](10737.md), [Wallis and Futuna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wallis_and_Futuna); Japanese in the [Bonin Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bonin_Islands);[^cite_note-realm-69] and [Spanish](26825.md) on [Easter Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easter_Island) and the [Galápagos Islands](171118.md).[^cite_note-realm-69][^cite_note-ling-126][^cite_note-381] There are also [Creoles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creole_language) formed from the interaction of Malay or the colonial languages with indigenous languages, such as [Tok Pisin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tok_Pisin), [Bislama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bislama), [Chavacano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chavacano), various [Malay trade and creole languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malay_trade_and_creole_languages), [Hawaiian Pidgin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawaiian_Pidgin), [Norfuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfuk), and [Pitkern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pitkern). Contact between Austronesian and Papuan resulted in several instances in mixed languages such as [Maisin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maisin_language).

### Immigration

Main articles: [Post-war immigration to Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-war_immigration_to_Australia), [Immigration to Australia](1100670.md), [Immigration to New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_to_New_Zealand), [Refugees in Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_in_Australia), [Refugees in New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refugees_in_New_Zealand), and [Kanaka (Pacific Island worker)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanaka_\(Pacific_Island_worker\))



[Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_Australians) immigrants arriving in Melbourne, Australia in 1954

The most multicultural areas in Oceania, which have a [high degree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_and_dependent_territories_by_immigrant_population) of [immigration](4599312.md), are Australia, New Zealand, and Hawaii. Since 1945, more than 7 million people have settled in Australia. From the late 1970s, there was a significant increase in immigration from Asian and other non-European countries, making Australia a [multicultural country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiculturalism_in_Australia).[^cite_note-bureau181126-382]

Sydney is the most [multicultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiculturalism) city in Oceania, having more than 250 different languages spoken, with about 40% of residents speaking a [language other than English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_Other_Than_English) at home.[^cite_note-herald231114-383] Furthermore, 36 percent of the population reported having been [born overseas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_born), with top countries being Italy, Lebanon, Vietnam, and Iraq, among others.[^cite_note-TheAus-384][^cite_note-Population,_dwellings,_and_ethnicity-385] [Melbourne](17306237.md) is also fairly multicultural, having the largest [Greek-speaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_community_of_Melbourne) population outside of Europe,[^cite_note-oecd5024-386] and the second largest [Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Australians) population in Australia after Sydney.[^cite_note-indiandir-387][^cite_note-srilandir-388][^cite_note-yarra062408-389]

European migration to New Zealand provided a major influx following the signing of the [Treaty of Waitangi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Waitangi) in 1840. Subsequent immigration has been chiefly from the British Isles, but also from continental Europe, the Pacific, the Americas, and Asia.[^cite_note-parlnz1840-390][^cite_note-immireg-391] [Auckland](18660332.md) is home to over half (51.6 percent) of New Zealand's overseas born population, including 72 percent of the country's Pacific Island-born population, 64 percent of its [Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_people)-born population, and 56 percent of its [Middle Eastern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_the_Middle_East) and African born population.[^cite_note-2013census-392]



Many [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Americans) immigrants in Hawaii were [Azorean](3226.md) or [Madeiran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madeira).

Hawaii is a [majority-minority state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majority-minority_state).[^cite_note-reuter013113-393] [Chinese](372983.md) workers on Western trading ships settled in Hawaii starting in 1789. In 1820, the first American missionaries arrived to preach Christianity and teach the Hawaiians Western ways.[^cite_note-Charles_Williams-394] As of 2015, a large proportion of Hawaii's population have Asian ancestry – especially [Filipino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipino_people), Japanese, [Korean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_immigration_to_Hawaii) and Chinese. Many are descendants of immigrants brought to work on the sugarcane plantations in the mid-to-late 19th century. Almost 13,000 [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_people) immigrants had arrived by 1899; they also worked on the [sugarcane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugarcane) plantations.[^cite_note-Hoffman-395] [Puerto Rican immigration to Hawaii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerto_Rican_immigration_to_Hawaii) began in 1899 when Puerto Rico's sugar industry was devastated by two hurricanes, causing a worldwide shortage of sugar and a huge demand for sugar from Hawaii.[^cite_note-ricandias-396]

Between 2001 and 2007, Australia's [Pacific Solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Solution) policy transferred [asylum seekers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asylum_seekers) to several Pacific nations, including the [Nauru detention centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nauru_detention_centre). Australia, New Zealand, and other nations took part in the [Regional Assistance Mission to Solomon Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_Assistance_Mission_to_Solomon_Islands) between 2003 and 2017 after a request for aid.[^cite_note-bat-397]

### Archaeogenetics



[Yali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yali_people) in the [Yahukimo Regency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahukimo_Regency), [Highland Papua](71191966.md)

[Archaeology](18951655.md), [linguistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linguistics), and existing [genetic studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genetics) indicate that Oceania was settled by two major waves of migration. The first migration of [Australo-Melanesians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australo-Melanesians) took place c. 40 to 80 thousand years ago, and these migrants, [Papuans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_people_of_New_Guinea), colonised much of [Near Oceania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Near_Oceania). Approximately 3.5 thousand years ago, a second expansion of [Austronesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austronesian_languages) speakers arrived in Near Oceania, and the descendants of these people spread to the far corners of the Pacific, colonising [Remote Oceania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remote_Oceania).[^cite_note-doi10.1016/j.ajhg.2014.03.014-398] [Mitochondrial DNA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitochondrial_DNA) (mtDNA) studies quantify the magnitude of the [Austronesian expansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austronesian_peoples) and demonstrate the homogenising effect of this expansion. With regards to Papuan influence, autochthonous [haplogroups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haplogroup) support the hypothesis of a long history in Near Oceania, with some lineages suggesting a time depth of 60 thousand years. [Santa Cruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Cruz_Islands), a population located in Remote Oceania, is an anomaly with extreme frequencies of autochthonous haplogroups of Near Oceanian origin.[^cite_note-doi10.1016/j.ajhg.2014.03.014-398]

Large areas of [New Guinea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Guinea) are unexplored by scientists and [anthropologists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthropologists) due to extensive [deforestation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deforestation) and mountainous terrain. Known indigenous tribes in [Papua New Guinea](22943.md) have very little contact with local authorities aside from the authorities knowing who they are. Many remain [preliterate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preliterate) and, at the national or international level, the names of tribes and information about them are extremely hard to obtain. The [Indonesian](14579.md) provinces of [Papua](15199.md) and [West Papua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Papua_\(province\)) on the island of New Guinea are home to an estimated 44 [uncontacted tribal groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uncontacted_peoples).[^cite_note-survival-399]

## Culture

### Australia

Main articles: [Culture of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Australia) and [Cuisine of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_Australia)



On 28 June 2007, the Sydney Opera House became a [UNESCO](21786641.md) [World Heritage Site](44940.md).

[^cite_note-operawin-400]

Since 1788, the primary influence behind Australian culture has been [Anglo-Celtic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Celtic) [Western culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_culture), with some [Indigenous](12598742.md) influences.[^cite_note-jupp769-401][^cite_note-twhite118-402] The divergence and evolution that has occurred in the ensuing centuries has resulted in a distinctive Australian culture.[^cite_note-Davison_pp98–9-403][^cite_note-twhite125-404] Since the mid-20th century, [American popular culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_the_United_States) has strongly influenced Australia, particularly through television and cinema.[^cite_note-tw-405] Other cultural influences come from neighbouring Asian countries, and through large-scale immigration from non-English-speaking nations.[^cite_note-tw-405][^cite_note-jupp74-406] *[The Story of the Kelly Gang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Story_of_the_Kelly_Gang)* (1906), the world's first [feature length](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feature_length) film, spurred a boom in [Australian cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Australia) during the [silent film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silent_film) era.[^cite_note-kellyg07-407][^cite_note-wavefilm-408] The [Australian Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Museum) in Sydney and the [National Gallery of Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Gallery_of_Victoria) in Melbourne are the oldest and [largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_art_museums) [museums](37585.md) in Oceania.[^cite_note-ausmuseum-409][^cite_note-VHR-410] The city's [New Year's Eve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_New_Year's_Eve) celebrations are the largest in Oceania.[^cite_note-auseve2013-411]

Australia is also known for its [cafe](370815.md) and [coffee culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee_culture) in [urban centres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_area).[^cite_note-abcnet160511-412] Australia and [New Zealand](4913064.md) were responsible for the [flat white](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flat_white) coffee. Most Indigenous Australian tribal groups subsisted on a simple [hunter-gatherer diet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter-gatherer_diet) of native fauna and flora, otherwise called [bush tucker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bush_tucker).[^cite_note-bushtpb-413] The first settlers introduced [British food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_cuisine) to the continent, much of which is now considered typical Australian food, such as the [Sunday roast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunday_roast).[^cite_note-food-414][^cite_note-f2-415] Multicultural immigration transformed Australian cuisine; post-World War II European migrants, particularly from the Mediterranean, helped to build a thriving Australian [coffee culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee_culture), and the influence of [Asian cultures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Asia) has led to Australian variants of their staple foods, such as the [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_cuisine)-inspired [dim sim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dim_sim) and [Chiko Roll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiko_Roll).[^cite_note-kangcww1999-416]

### Hawaii

Main articles: [Culture of Hawaii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Hawaii), [Cuisine of Hawaii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_Hawaii), and [Hawaiian religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawaiian_religion)



The [ʻIolani Palace](469613.md) in [Honolulu](13887.md), formerly the residence of the Hawaiian monarch, was restored and opened to the public as a museum in 1978.

The music of Hawaii includes traditional and popular styles, ranging from native Hawaiian folk music to modern [rock](25423.md) and [hip-hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hip-hop). Hawaii's musical contributions to the [music of the United States](171080.md) are out of proportion to the state's small size. Styles such as [slack-key guitar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slack-key_guitar) are well known worldwide, while Hawaiian-tinged music is a frequent part of [Hollywood](418334.md) soundtracks. Hawaii also made a major contribution to [country music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_music) with the introduction of the [steel guitar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steel_guitar).[^cite_note-Unterberger-417] The Hawaiian religion is [polytheistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polytheism) and [animistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animism), with a belief in many deities and spirits, including the belief that spirits are found in non-human beings and objects such as animals, the waves, and the sky.[^cite_note-Chai-418]

The [cuisine of Hawaii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_Hawaii) is a fusion of many foods brought by immigrants to the Hawaiian Islands, including the earliest Polynesians and [native Hawaiians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Hawaiian_cuisine), and [American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_the_United_States), [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_cuisine), [Filipino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipino_cuisine), [Japanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_cuisine), [Korean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_cuisine), [Polynesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polynesian_cuisine), and [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_cuisine) origins. Native Hawaiian musician and Hawaiian sovereignty activist [Israel Kamakawiwoʻole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Kamakawiwo%CA%BBole), famous for his medley of "[Somewhere Over the Rainbow/What a Wonderful World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somewhere_Over_the_Rainbow/What_a_Wonderful_World)", was named "The Voice of Hawaii" by [NPR](29697232.md) in 2010 in its 50 great voices series.[^cite_note-npr101206-419]

### New Zealand

Main articles: [Culture of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_New_Zealand) and [Cuisine of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_New_Zealand)

Hills with inset, round doors. Reflected in water.

The [Hobbiton Movie Set](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hobbiton_Movie_Set), located near [Matamata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matamata), was used for *The Lord of the Rings* film trilogy.

New Zealand, as a culture, is a Western culture, which is influenced by the cultural input of the indigenous [Māori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C4%81ori_people) and the various waves of multi-ethnic migration which followed the [British colonisation of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_New_Zealand). The Māori people constitute one of the major cultures of Polynesia. The country has been broadened by globalisation and immigration, specifically from Oceania, Europe, and Asia.[^cite_note-natdays-421] New Zealand marks two national days of remembrance, [Waitangi Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waitangi_Day) and [ANZAC Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ANZAC_Day), and also celebrates many holidays such as the King's Birthday, Labour Day, and Christmas Day, as well as public anniversaries of the founding dates of most regions.[^cite_note-govnathol-422] The New Zealand recording industry began to develop from 1940 onwards and many New Zealand musicians have obtained success in Britain and the United States.[^cite_note-music-423] Some artists release Māori language songs and the Māori tradition-based art of *[kapa haka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kapa_haka)* (song and dance) has made a resurgence.[^cite_note-create062010-424] The country's diverse scenery and compact size, plus government incentives,[^cite_note-nyt101028-425] have encouraged some [producers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_producer) to film big budget movies in New Zealand, including *[Avatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avatar_\(2009_film\))*, *[The Lord of the Rings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Lord_of_the_Rings_\(film_series\))*, *[The Hobbit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hobbit_\(film_series\))*, *[The Chronicles of Narnia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Chronicles_of_Narnia_\(film_series\))*, *[King Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Kong_\(2005_film\))* and *[The Last Samurai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Last_Samurai)*.[^cite_note-filmnz-426]

The national cuisine has been described as [Pacific Rim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Rim), incorporating the native [Māori cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C4%81ori_cuisine) and diverse culinary traditions introduced by settlers and immigrants from Europe, Polynesia, and Asia.[^cite_note-cuisine-427] New Zealand yields produce from land and sea – most crops and livestock, such as maize, potatoes, and pigs, were gradually introduced by the early European settlers.[^cite_note-kaipakeha-428] Distinctive ingredients or dishes include [lamb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamb_and_mutton); salmon; [koura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paranephrops) (crayfish);[^cite_note-encyclnzshell-429] [whitebait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitebait); shellfish including [dredge oysters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dredge_oyster), [pāua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P%C4%81ua), mussels, scallops, [pipi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paphies_australis) and [tuatua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuatua);[^cite_note-ecyclnzshell222-430] [kumara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweet_potato) (sweet potato); [kiwifruit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiwifruit); [tamarillo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamarillo); and [pavlova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pavlova_\(food\)) (considered a national dish).[^cite_note-encyclnzcook-431][^cite_note-cuisine-427]

### Samoa

Main articles: [Culture of Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Samoa) and [Polynesian culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polynesian_culture)



A [fale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Samoa) on [Manono Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manono_Island)

The [fa'a Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fa'a_Samoa), or traditional Samoan way, remains a strong force in Samoan life and politics. Despite centuries of European influence, Samoa maintains its historical customs, social and political systems, and [language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samoan_language). Cultural customs such as the [Samoa 'ava ceremony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samoa_'ava_ceremony) are significant and solemn rituals at important occasions, including the bestowal of *[matai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fa'amatai)* chiefly titles. Items of great cultural value include the finely woven *['ie toga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/'ie_toga)*. The Samoan word for dance is *[siva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siva_Samoa)*, which consists of unique, gentle movements of the body in time to music and which tell a story. Samoan male dances can be more snappy.[^cite_note-dancesiva-432] The *[sasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sasa_\(dance\))* is also a traditional dance where rows of dancers perform rapid [synchronised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synchronization) movements in time to the rhythm of wooden drums *([pate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pate_\(musical_instrument\)))* or rolled mats. Another dance performed by males is called the *[fa'ataupati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fa'ataupati)* or the slap dance, creating rhythmic sounds by slapping different parts of the body. As with other Polynesian cultures ([Hawaiian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawaiian_culture), [Tahitian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahitians), and [Māori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C4%81ori_culture)) with significant and unique tattoos, Samoans have two gender specific and culturally significant tattoos.[^cite_note-wwpride-433]

### Arts

Main articles: [Oceanian art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceanian_art), [Samoan art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samoan_art), [Hawaiian art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawaiian_art), [Māori art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C4%81ori_art), and [Indigenous Australian art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_Australian_art)



[Gwion Gwion rock paintings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gwion_Gwion_rock_paintings) found in the north-west [Kimberley region of Western Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimberley_region_of_Western_Australia)

The artistic creations of native Oceanians vary greatly throughout the cultures and regions. The subject matter typically carries themes of fertility or the supernatural. [Petroglyphs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petroglyphs), [tattooing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tattooing), painting, wood carving, stone carving, and textile work are other common art forms.[^cite_note-artino-434] Art of Oceania properly encompasses the artistic traditions of the people indigenous to Australia and the Pacific Islands.[^cite_note-oce-435] These early peoples lacked a [writing system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Writing_system), and made works on perishable materials, so few records of them exist from this time.[^cite_note-mettime-436] Indigenous Australian [rock art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_art) is the oldest and richest unbroken tradition of art in the world, dating as far back as 60,000 years and spread across hundreds of thousands of sites.[^cite_note-rockart531-437][^cite_note-nyt051106-438] These rock paintings served several functions. Some were used in magic, others to increase animal populations for hunting, while some were simply for amusement.[^cite_note-mettime4-439] Sculpture in Oceania first appears on New Guinea as a series of stone figures found throughout the island, but mostly in the mountainous highlands. Establishing a chronological timeframe for these pieces in most cases is difficult, but one has been dated to c. 1500 BCE.[^cite_note-mettime444-440]

By 1500 BCE, the [Lapita culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lapita_culture), descendants of the second wave, would begin to expand and spread into the more remote islands. At around the same time, art began to appear in New Guinea, including the earliest examples of sculpture in Oceania. Beginning c. 1100 CE, the people of Easter Island would begin construction of nearly 900 [moai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moai) (large stone statues). At c. 1200 CE, the people of Pohnpei, a Micronesian island, would embark on another megalithic construction, building [Nan Madol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nan_Madol), a city of artificial islands and a system of canals.[^cite_note-mettime_453-441] Hawaiian art includes [wood carvings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wood_carving), feather work, petroglyphs, bark cloth (called [kapa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kapa) in Hawaiian and [tapa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tapa_cloth) elsewhere in the Pacific), and tattoos. Native Hawaiians had neither metal nor woven cloth.[^cite_note-mettime3456-442]

### Sport

See also: [Sport in Oceania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Oceania)



Fiji playing [Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wales_national_rugby_sevens_team) at [seven-a-side rugby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rugby_sevens)

[Rugby union](25405.md) is one of the region's most prominent sports,[^cite_note-ocerugb-443] and is the national sport of New Zealand, Samoa, Fiji, and Tonga. The most popular overall sport in Australia is [cricket](25675557.md), with their [national team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_national_cricket_team) having won the [Cricket World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cricket_World_Cup) a record five times.[^cite_note-444] The most popular sport among Australian women is [netball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netball), while [Australian rules football](2403.md) garners the highest spectatorship numbers and television ratings.[^cite_note-ausrulebrit-445][^cite_note-aust54-446][^cite_note-planetsp85-447][^cite_note-aust101-448] Rugby union is the most popular sport among New Zealanders,[^cite_note-newzea76-449] and they are second only to [South Africa](17416221.md) for the most [Rugby World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rugby_World_Cup) titles, having won the tournament three times.[^cite_note-450] Australia's team [the Wallabies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_national_rugby_union_team) have won the World Cup twice, despite Rugby union being less popular among Australians.[^cite_note-451] In Papua New Guinea, the most popular sport is [Rugby league](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rugby_league).[^cite_note-rl-452][^cite_note-rugby66-453] [Fiji's sevens team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiji_national_rugby_sevens_team) is one of the most successful in the world, as is [New Zealand's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_national_rugby_sevens_team).[^cite_note-southemiclass-454]

Australian rules football is the national sport in [Nauru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_rules_football_in_Nauru).[^cite_note-naurafl-455] It has a large following in Papua New Guinea, where it is the second most popular sport after Rugby League.[^cite_note-miningpapua-456][^cite_note-msnclosure-457][^cite_note-footinaus-458] Additionally, it attracts significant attention across New Zealand and the [Pacific Islands](341111.md). The highest level of the sport is the [Australian Football League](4732785.md) (AFL), which was the fourth-best attended sporting league in the world during the 2010s.[^cite_note-459]

[Vanuatu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_Vanuatu) is the only country in Oceania to call [association football](10568.md) its national sport. However, it is also the most popular sport in [Kiribati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_Kiribati), [Solomon Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_Solomon_Islands) and [Tuvalu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_Tuvalu), and has a significant (and growing) popularity in [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soccer_in_Australia). In 2006, Australia left the [Oceania Football Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceania_Football_Confederation) (OFC) for the [Asian Football Confederation](715249.md) (AFC), and their men's team [the Socceroos](4792249.md) has qualified for every subsequent [FIFA World Cup](11370.md) as an Asian entrant.[^cite_note-fifa2010-460] The sole Micronesian country with membership in the OFC is Kiribati, although it is not recognised by [FIFA](11049.md) like the other OFC members. [Federated States of Micronesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federated_States_of_Micronesia_national_football_team), Marshall Islands, Nauru and [Palau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palau_national_football_team) all have no presence, primarily due to lack of infrastructure and logistical difficulties related to Micronesia's remoteness.[^cite_note-461][^cite_note-Associate_Members_of_OFC-462][^cite_note-463] Like Australia, the Micronesian dependent territories of [Guam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guam_national_football_team) and [Northern Mariana Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Mariana_Islands_national_football_team) currently compete in the AFC instead of the OFC.[^cite_note-464] The OFC was dominated by Australia for many years, and became known for one-sided results.[^cite_note-wsc-465]

Australians view sport as an important part of their cultural identity, and the country performs well on the international stage, despite having a relatively small population.[^cite_note-466] They have hosted two Summer Olympics: [Melbourne 1956](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1956_Summer_Olympics) and [Sydney 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_Summer_Olympics), and the city of Brisbane is also set to host the [2032 edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2032_Summer_Olympics).[^cite_note-467] Australia has hosted five editions of the [Commonwealth Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_Games) and New Zealand three times. The [Pacific Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Games) (formerly known as the South Pacific Games) is a multi-sport event, much like the Olympics on a much smaller scale, with participation exclusively from countries around the Pacific; Australia and New Zealand competed in the games for the first time in 2015.[^cite_note-abc20140703-468]

Melbourne hosts the [Australian Open](97851.md) every year, considered one of the four [Grand Slam](197638.md) tournaments in [tennis](29773.md).[^cite_note-469]

[ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [978-0-19-870159-0](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/978-0-19-870159-0). The region of Oceania describes a collection of islands scattered throughout the Pacific Ocean between Asia and the Americas. The region is vast and largely covered by the ocean. There are four subregions of this region, including Australasia (Australia and New Zealand), Melanesia (Fiji, New Caledonia, Papua New Guinea, Solomon Islands, Vanuatu, and [Western New Guinea](1432822.md)), Micronesia (the Federated States of Micronesia, Guam, the Marshall Islands, Nauru, and Palau etc.), and Polynesia (American Samoa, the Cook Islands, French Polynesia, Niue, Samoa, Tokelau, Tonga, and Tuvalu etc.).

[^cite_note-64]: &#x20;"Although based on a superficial understanding of the Pacific islanders, Dumont d'Urville's tripartite classification stuck. Indeed, these categories—Polynesians, Micronesians, Melanesians—became so deeply entrenched in Western anthropological thought that it is difficult even now to break out of the mould in which they entrap us ([Thomas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicholas_Thomas_\(anthropologist\)), 1989). Such labels provide handy geographical referents, yet they mislead us greatly if we take them to be meaningful segments of cultural history. Only Polynesia has stood the tests of time and increased knowledge, as a category with historical significance" —[Patrick Vinton Kirch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Vinton_Kirch), *On the Road of the Winds: An Archeological History of the Pacific Islands Before European Contact*, Berkeley, University of California Press, 2000: 5.

[^cite_note-realm-69]: &#x20;Todd, Ian (1974). [*Island Realm: A Pacific Panorama*](https://books.google.com/books?id=gcEJAQAAIAAJ\&q=%22French+language+cultures%22+1974+pacific). Angus & Robertson. p. 190. [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [978-0-207-12761-8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/978-0-207-12761-8). [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220618161036/https://www.google.com.au/books/edition/Island_Realm/gcEJAQAAIAAJ?hl=en\&gbpv=1\&bsq=%22French+language+cultures%22+1974+pacific\&dq=%22French+language+cultures%22+1974+pacific\&printsec=frontcover) from the original on 18 June 2022. Retrieved 2 February 2022. \[we] can further define the word *culture* to mean *language*. Thus, we have the French language part of Oceania, the Spanish part, and the Japanese part. The Japanese culture groups of Oceania are the Bonin Islands, the Marcus Islands, and the Volcano Islands. These three clusters, lying south and south-east of Japan, are inhabited either by Japanese or by people who have now completely fused with the Japanese race. Therefore, they will not be taken into account in the proposed comparison of the policies of non-Oceanic cultures towards Oceanic peoples. On the eastern side of the Pacific are several Spanish-language culture groups of islands. Two of them, the Galapagos and Easter Island, have been dealt with as separate chapters in this volume. Only one of the dozen or so Spanish culture island groups of Oceania has an Oceanic population — the Polynesians of Easter Island. The rest are either uninhabited or have a Spanish - Latin - American population consisting of people who migrated from the mainland. Therefore, the comparisons that follow refer almost exclusively to the English and French language cultures.

[^cite_note-chambers-95]: &#x20;[*Chambers's New Handy Volume American Encyclopædia: Volume 9*](https://books.google.com/books?id=J2NRAAAAYAAJ\&dq=%22easter+island%22+%22oceania%22+%22galapagos%22\&pg=PA657). The University of Virginia. 1885. p. 657. [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220730064400/https://www.google.com.au/books/edition/Chambers_s_New_Handy_Volume_American_Enc/J2NRAAAAYAAJ?hl=en\&gbpv=1\&dq=%22easter+island%22+%22oceania%22+%22galapagos%22\&pg=PA657\&printsec=frontcover) from the original on 30 July 2022. Retrieved 13 March 2022. the whole region has sometimes been called Oceania, and sometimes Australasia—generally, however, in modern times, to the exclusion of the islands in the Indian archipelago, to which certain writers have given the name of Malaysia \[...] we have the three geographical divisions of Malesia, Australasia and Polynesia, the last mentioned of which embraces all the groups and single islands not included under the other two. Accepting this arrangement, still, the limits between Australasia and Polynesia have not been very accurately defined; indeed, scarcely any two geographers appear to be quite agreed upon the subject; neither shall we pretend to decide in the matter. The following list, however, comprises all the principal groups and single islands not previously named as coming under the division of Australasia: 1. North of the equator—The Ladrone or Marian islands, the Pelew Islands, the Caroline Islands, the Radack and Ralick chains, the Sandwich islands, Gilbert's or Kingstnill's archipelago, and the Galapagos. 2. South of the equator—The Ellice group, the Phoenix and Union groups, the Fiji Islands, the Friendly Islands, the Navigator's islands, Cook's or Harvey islands, the Society islands, the Dangerous archipelago, the Marquesas Islands, Pitcairn Island, and Easter Island.

[*Current Trends in Linguistics: Linguistics in Oceania*](https://books.google.com/books?id=lYouAAAAMAAJ\&q=%22multitude%22+oceania+%22linguistics%22). the University of Michigan. p. 950. [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20220730065403/https://www.google.com.au/books/edition/Current_Trends_in_Linguistics_Linguistic/lYouAAAAMAAJ?hl=en\&gbpv=1\&bsq=%22multitude%22+oceania+%22linguistics%22\&dq=%22multitude%22+oceania+%22linguistics%22\&printsec=frontcover) from the original on 30 July 2022. Retrieved 2 February 2022. Most of this account of the influence of the Hispanic languages in Oceania has dealt with the Western Pacific, but the Eastern Pacific has not been without some share of the presence of the Portuguese and Spanish. The Eastern Pacific does not have the multitude of islands so characteristic of the Western regions of this great ocean, but there are some: Easter Island, 2000 miles off the Chilean coast, where a Polynesian tongue, Rapanui, is still spoken; the Juan Fernandez group, 400 miles west of Valparaiso; the Galapagos archipelago, 650 miles west of Ecuador; Malpelo and Cocos, 300 miles off the Colombian and Costa Rican coasts respectively; and others. Not many of these islands have extensive populations — some have been used effectively as prisons — but the official language on each is Spanish.

Oceania is further grouped into three regions: Melanesia, Micronesia, and Polynesia. There are a few other Pacific island groups that do not fit into these groupings, such as the Galapagos.

[^cite_note-vikings228-209]: &#x20;Resemblance of the name to an early [Mangarevan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangareva) founder god *Atu Motua* ("Father Lord") has made some historians suspect that Hotu Matua was added to Easter Island mythology only in the 1860s, along with adopting the Mangarevan language. The "real" founder would have been *Tu'u ko Iho*, who became just a supporting character in Hotu Matuʻa centric legends. See Steven Fischer (1994). *Rapanui's Tu'u ko Iho Versus Mangareva's 'Atu Motua. Evidence for Multiple Reanalysis and Replacement in Rapanui Settlement Traditions, Easter Island*.
